| D-061 | 2026-09-24 | W4-C audio engine (`src/media/audio.ts`). Each audio-bearing asset is decoded once, whole, with an OfflineAudioContext (files over 512 MB are skipped). Detached audio decodes its video's bytes. Audible clips: audio-track clips and video clips not `audioDetached`, enabled, on unmuted tracks, and on soloed tracks when any track is soloed; frozen clips are silent. On play, seek, any project or solo change, or more than 50 ms of drift, every source stops and is rescheduled from the transport's continuous clock, as `AudioBufferSourceNode`s at `playbackRate = speed`. Reversed clips use a reversed buffer copy. Start offsets are advanced by `baseLatency + outputLatency`. Playing videos are steered toward the same clock by nudging `playbackRate` up to ±20% past half a frame of drift. Paused scrubs play an 80 ms snippet, throttled with a trailing snippet. Waveform peaks (100 per second, bytes) are cached as `waves/<fp>` and drawn normalised to the file's loudest peak with the `--color-waveform` token. The test hook gains a read-only `getMedia()` snapshot.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Sample-accurate Web Audio scheduling plus rate steering keeps sound and picture within one frame (measured gaps of 13–21 ms in the sandbox) without WebCodecs. | D-064 | 2026-09-24 | W5-A export architecture. `src/export/worker.ts` (a module worker) plans each output frame's media requests with `deriveRenderItems`. It decodes each video layer's frames with Mediabunny `VideoSampleSink.samplesAtTimestamps` (WebCodecs, frame-accurate, independent of playback) and draws each frame with the preview's `drawComposition` (`overlays: false`, black letterbox) on an OffscreenCanvas at output size. It encodes with Mediabunny `CanvasSource` and `AudioSampleSource`, and streams to OPFS `aive-media/exports/<id>.<ext>` (BufferTarget when OPFS is missing). Main thread: pre-flight (EXP-008); images decoded to ImageBitmaps (workers cannot decode SVG); the audio mix rendered by an OfflineAudioContext at 48 kHz stereo with the playback clip list and `clipSchedule` (`listAudibleClips` moved to `src/media/audio.ts` and shared). MP4 (H.264 + AAC) when the worker's `canEncodeVideo('avc')` and `canEncodeAudio('aac')` both succeed, else WebM (VP9 + Opus); the dialog shows which, and why. Quality maps to Mediabunny's resolution-aware Low, Medium or High. Only the latest export file is kept in OPFS. Solo is a monitoring aid and is ignored by export; mute is respected. The dialog is modal, and closing it cancels the export.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | One drawing path keeps preview and export matching (EXP-007). WebCodecs decode follows D-005. | D-160 | 2026-10-03 | J7 lane model. Every track is a lane in one of three groups, top to bottom: text and shapes (track types `text` and `object`), visuals (`video`) and audio (`audio`); `src/core/lanes.ts` holds the rule. A layer's group: video, image and backgrounds (a `role: background` string property, set on library backgrounds, their pieces and template backdrops; schema unchanged) are visuals; text, shapes, drawings and groups of them are text and shapes; a group holding a picture is a visual; audio is audio. The validator checks the group only (text-and-shape lanes hold text, shapes and groups; visual lanes hold pictures, shapes and groups; audio lanes audio), so earlier documents stay valid. Lane order is the stacking order: after every track or clip command (CREATE_TRACK, DELETE_TRACK, MOVE_TRACK, CREATE_CLIP, MOVE_CLIP) the core sorts the lanes by group, keeping their order within a group, and puts the top-level layers in lane order (the top lane in front; layers on one lane, which never overlap, keep their order; layers without a clip stay at the back). A new lane opens at the top of its group (CREATE_TRACK then MOVE_TRACK), so a new element is in front of its kind. Audio has one lane: a new sound goes to the nearest free time there; detached audio keeps its time (sync) and opens a second audio lane only when the first is busy then. On open, a clip on a lane of another group moves to a lane of its own group; a document with no clips (the example, a template) gets lanes packed from its stacking order (an overlapping layer behind goes to a lower lane), and a document with clips keeps its lanes, its stacking following them. Arrange (CV-026) moves top-level elements between the lanes of their group (forward and backward one lane, front and back to the group's top and bottom; a busy lane gets a new lane next to it; an emptied lane is deleted), so text can never go behind a picture by Arrange. A drag over a lane of another group is refused: not-allowed cursor, hatched lane, nothing changes on release. Ungroup ignores the `role` marker. Unit tests that stated the old rules now state these: TL-001 expects the cards group on a text-and-shapes lane and an all-clip document to keep its lanes with the layers in lane order; KEY-001 picks the lower-lane layer for Bring forward and to front. LCR: TL-061, TL-062, TL-063.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Owner J7 brief (Clipchamp lanes). Keeping the layers array as the one stacking store, re-sorted by the core after lane changes, keeps every renderer, hit test and panel unchanged. Keeping a saved document's lanes, rather than repacking them, keeps the timeline the user knows.                                                                                                                 | Approved                            |

| D-165 | 2026-10-03 | J12 transitions, schema 6 unchanged. A transition is stored on the incoming clip's existing `transitionMetadata` as `in: {type, duration}` (`src/core/transitions.ts`), set by the new additive command `SET_CLIP_TRANSITION` (null removes it): it is refused unless a clip on the same lane ends exactly where this one starts, and its duration (0.1 to 5 s) may not exceed twice either clip's length. It plays only while the clips touch, over a window centred on the cut. Drawing (`src/render/transitions.ts`, after the presets, for the preview and the export worker alike; picking and editing use the resting state): over the window both clips are shown, the outgoing one held on its last frame after the cut and the incoming one on its first frame before it (`freezeFrame`), the incoming one drawn above. Cross fade: the incoming clip fades in over the outgoing one, which stays whole (for opaque pictures exactly (1 − p)·A + p·B; text being replaced disappears at the end of the window). Fade through black or white: a full-canvas veil peaking at the cut, the outgoing clip until the cut and the incoming after. Wipe left and right: the incoming clip is revealed from the right or left edge (a reveal side was added to the W5-C wipe). Slide left and right: both move by the canvas width. Progress is eased in and out. Audio is not crossfaded. The Transition panel is a left side panel (search, six groups, Duration NumberField clamped with a message, Remove) opened by the + on a touching cut or the chip on a cut with a transition. The Transitions rail browse panel (TR-010) is unchanged. | D-169 | 2026-10-04 | T1 drag and drop. Root cause, as far as the sandbox Chromium could show (the owner's stuck state on Windows Chrome did not reproduce here after 30+ mixed drags): every in-app drag used the browser's HTML5 drag and drop, which kept three defects. (1) Media card thumbnails are `<img>` elements, which browsers make draggable by default; D-138 (I1) set `draggable=false` only on library cards. Once a file's thumbnail is cached in the media store, which outlives a refresh and a new project, a press on the picture could start a native drag of the image instead of the card. (2) The import overlay counted every dragleave but only file dragenters, and its drop handler ran in the bubble phase, so a file dropped on the Media drop zone (which stops the event) left the full-window overlay up, catching every later drag. (3) The drag record behind the J9 drop box was cleared only by the card's own dragend, which a re-rendered card never receives. Fix: one pointer-event drag controller (`src/ui/drag-controller.ts`) for every in-app source. Sources are `draggable=false` and so are their images (a dragstart from one is cancelled). The controller draws a ghost that follows the pointer and asks the registered drop targets (canvas stage, timeline lanes, Media folders) at each move. It ends on release, Escape, pointer cancel, window blur or a hidden page, and a drag never also clicks the card. A target that refuses can still say why (MED-013). The overlay is drawn but never takes the pointer, counts file drags only, and is reset by any drop (capture phase), dragend or leaving the window. Dropped files are imported and, over the canvas or the timeline, also placed there. A single click on a Media card now adds it (canvas centre, clip at the playhead). Library inserts take an optional clip start time for timeline drops. Timeline clip drags also cancel on a hidden page. The canvas target is the whole stage, so a drop onto the selection's action cluster still lands. The TPL-012 test that asserted the card's HTML5 `draggable=true` now asserts it is a controller source and not natively draggable. Its intent is unchanged: the card drags, the image does not.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Owner T1 brief: find the real root cause and make every drag source use one controller. A pointer-driven drag has no browser drag session, drag image or dataTransfer negotiation that can get stuck. OS files still need HTML5 drag and drop, because that is the only way a page receives them.                                                                                                    | Approved                            |

Core: after every transaction the engine deletes a lane that had clips before it and has none after (`removeEmptiedLanes`, in the same history entry), so Undo restores it; lanes that were already empty (fixtures, a fresh track) are left. | D-189 | 2026-10-09 | T-ALL P6 FX wiring, schema 7 unchanged. A clip's stack is `clip.metadata.fx = {version: 1, stack: [{id, params}] (at most 16), blendMode?}` (the FX-LIBRARY.md convention, read defensively by `clipFx`), set by the new additive command `SET_CLIP_FX` (refused on locked lanes; an empty stack with a normal blend deletes the field, so round trips restore the document). Transitions accept a `transition.*` library id and optional params in the existing `transitionMetadata.in`. Right panel: Filters lists the 47 filters by category (Original first, one filter per clip, an intensity field); Effects lists the 28 effects (a click adds or removes one, each with an intensity field; shapes and text get them in an accordion beside the outline and shadow); Adjust colors has live Exposure, Contrast, Saturation and Temperature (−100 to 100, 0 removes), a Blend mode select (the 16 library modes) and Reset. Each change is one undo step on every selected clip. The Transition panel maps its planned entries to library transitions and adds a More group with the rest. Rendering: `src/render/fx.ts` paints an affected item alone on a scratch OffscreenCanvas at the output's pixel size, reads it back, runs `renderStack` with clip-local time and a seed from the clip id (FNV-1a), and draws it back once with the blend mode; a pixel transition paints both clips that way and mixes them where the incoming one is drawn. `drawComposition` calls it, so the preview, PNG frames and the export worker share one path. Group children use their top-level clip's stack, each child processed on its own. Limits: no thumbnails, hover previews or keyframable parameters (FX-001, FX-003 stay Todo); only the intensity and amount parameters have controls; CLR-001 and MSK-001 are Claimed (four of eleven colour controls; blend modes have no Add and are stored but not pixel-tested); CPU processing per frame, no real-time guarantee (FX-012); FX item names are English in Hindi too. Tests that asserted the planned controls now assert the live ones (LAY-042, LAY-045, LAY-047, TR-011). LCR: FX-015, TR-012.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Owner T-ALL brief, P6, and the FX-LIBRARY.md integration steps.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Approved                            |

| D-199 | 2026-10-11 | V7e scene-to-scene transitions (spec 10.5; B37), schema 7 unchanged. A composition has no free field, so the transition into a scene is one reserved marker at time 0 of the incoming scene whose label is 'aive:scene-transition ' plus the transition JSON (the clip transition schema). It is never drawn, snapped to, copied with a duplicated scene or a template, joined into an export's markers, or counted in a scene's length. Deleting a scene removes its incoming transition and the next scene's. Moving a scene keeps the transition with its incoming scene. It plays across the cut, half before and half after, like a clip transition (no scene shortened): both scenes are drawn whole, the other one held on its boundary frame, read back and mixed by the same pixel mix the transition tiles preview with (moved to src/render/scene-transitions.ts), so every tile works between scenes. The editor draws it while playing; the joined export draws it frame by frame (the worker plans each draw's video samples). | D-219 | 2026-10-12 | X1 frame cache (spec section 5). Frames are lossless PNG with alpha in the media store (OPFS, IndexedDB fallback) under `codeframes/<cacheKey>-<frame>`; an index `codeindex/index.json` records per key the frames stored from 0 on, the total, bytes and last use. The stored `bakeKey` hashes (cyrb53, two seeds, 28 hex characters) the tier, source, params with defaults, duration, seed and, when the code uses libraries, their versions; the cache key adds the pixel size, frame rate and quality. The host seed is a pure function of the source (the code's own seed param is the user's control), so two bakes are equal. Budgets: 384 MB of decoded frames in memory, 4 GB on disk, least recently used first (the open project's keys can be kept). Frames are baked in order from 0, one job at a time; a cancelled job keeps its frames, which play, and resumes only on Resume (a stateful animation replays from 0 without rewriting stored frames; a seekable one continues at the first missing frame). Quality (Full = scene size, Draft = half) is transient per-layer session state, not saved, because schema 8 allows one field only; export always uses Full.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Owner X1 brief. PNG keeps alpha exactly; deriving the seed keeps the schema to one field.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Approved                            |

| D-252 | 2026-10-15 | Q3 pivot (anchor) without changing the transform contract (the stored anchor stays at local (0, 0), TRANSFORM_CONTRACT.md). A layer may name a pivot with the string property `pivot` (top-left … bottom-right, centre; schema unchanged, read defensively); the Properties tab has an Anchor 9-point grid (centre shown by default; one 'Set anchor' step). With a named pivot and a box size (width and height properties), the Inspector's Rotation and Scale fields and the rotate handle turn about it, and animated rotation or scale is evaluated about it in `compositionAt` (the position moves so the pivot stays where the resting rotation and scale put it), so the canvas, picking and export agree. Without a named pivot nothing changes (rotation edits still turn about the visual centre, scale fields keep the corner). A Creator preset that animates rotation or scale sets the pivot to the centre when the layer has none, so Spin turns about the centre. Groups have no box size, so their pivot is not applied yet. Keyframe diamonds are 14 px glyphs in 20 px targets, outlined against the clip, lit on hover and accent-filled when selected. The timeline's redraw key now includes the editor mode (switching to 2D Animation after a preset showed no diamonds).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Owner QT brief, Q2 and Q3.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Approved                            |

| D-270 | 2026-10-17 | CAP captions engine (`src/captions`, docs/CAPTIONS-API.md). Speech is transcribed through an injected `SttProvider` (src/speech types) and cut into cues by the speech engine's own `toCaptionCues`, which gains one optional option, `maxLines` (default 2, so earlier callers are unchanged). Style presets are plain data: clean, bold-box, karaoke, lower-third and minimal (font, size at a 1080 px canvas, weight, colour, alignment, box, position, lines, characters per line, case, karaoke highlight). A caption track becomes ordinary layers through the Command Bus: one new text lane `Captions` at the top, a text layer per cue (in a group with a rounded box shape when the style has a box, since text has no outline or shadow yet, TXT-019) and its clip, so moves, trims, themes, animation and export just work. Each caption text layer keeps its cue in the string property `captionCue` (JSON: version, id, start, end, words, approximate, language, style id; schema unchanged, read defensively); `findCaptions` uses it with the clip's place, so a moved caption keeps its words in step. Editing a cue's text keeps its start and end (word timings kept when the word count is the same, else spread by length and marked approximate); `resplit` cuts a track again keeping every word's timing. Karaoke is an animated `textRuns` property (held string keyframes, D-070; rich text runs, J4): from each word's start the spoken words take the highlight colour and the current one is underlined; only colour and underline change, so the layout never moves. SRT and VTT export and import to the millisecond; VTT writes a WebVTT timestamp before each word so word timings come back (a word's end is read as the next word's start). `createCaptionsHook` is the composition adapter's captions hook (cues, a track, or STT and media transcribed first because the hook is synchronous): each cue goes to the scene it starts in, cut at the scene's end, with deterministic ids. No new dependency; no UI.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Owner FINAL1 brief, captions. Reusing the speech engine's cue rules and ordinary text layers keeps one caption model for the editor, the adapter and export.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Approved                            |
| D-271 | 2026-10-17 | NEST nested (compound) clips (`src/nesting`, `src/core/nesting.ts`, docs/NESTING-API.md), schema unchanged. A nested clip is a group layer with one clip whose `metadata.nested` (version 1, the inner duration, and each child's former clip with its start relative to the anchor and its lane id, name, type and rank) keeps what a flatten needs (D-033 pattern, read defensively). Chosen over a scene reference because a group with a clip already behaves as one clip on the timeline (D-039, D-251) and the canvas, export and outliner already draw groups; a scene source would need a new clip kind, a second playback path and a schema field. The children keep their times and keyframes in the nested clip's own frame, anchored at `startTime - sourceIn / speed` (at nesting this is the clip's start, so nothing changes). `nestedTimeAt` maps outer time to that frame through the outer clip's trim, speed, reverse and freeze (identity at speed 1 without trim). Core changes, all behind the `nested` record: `SET_CLIP_TIMING` on a nested clip shifts the children only by the anchor's change instead of D-251's edge retiming (a move carries them, a trim moves nothing); `SET_CLIP_SPEED` shifts them when a trimmed clip's anchor moves; `validateProject` leaves a nested clip's children out of the scene's length. The render adapter draws a nested clip's children at the inner time (keyframes evaluated there, their kept clips for media time), so the preview, picking, PNG frames and export follow; render items of every project fixture are hashed before and after and are identical. `nestClips` (GROUP, DELETE_CLIP, a new lane at the topmost selected lane's place, CREATE_CLIP) and `flattenNested` (the children back with their clips and lanes, timed through the outer trim and speed) are each one transaction; nest then flatten restores layers, timing and lanes. Depth over 5 and cycles are refused with an Error; audio cannot be nested yet; flattening a transformed, reversed or frozen nested clip is refused. Enter and exit are pure transient state helpers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Owner FINAL1 brief, nesting. Reusing the group keeps one clip model; the anchor frame makes moves and trims of the outer clip exact without rewriting the children.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Approved                            |
| D-272 | 2026-10-17 | FINAL1 scene camera (docs/CAMERA-API.md). A composition may hold an optional `camera` (number properties x, y, zoom, rotation keyframed with the existing model and easing, plus an optional seeded shake), set by the new additive command `SET_COMPOSITION_CAMERA` (null removes it). Like `playRange` (D-181) it is an optional field in schema 9: no version bump, and a scene without a camera draws byte for byte as before. `drawComposition` draws items through view × camera × item; the artboard, its clip and the editor overlays stay in view, and picking keeps the resting geometry (as D-072). Presets are data (`cameraPreset`: push in, pull out, four pans, Ken Burns, handheld). The joined export gets one camera made from each scene's, shifted and held to the cut (`joinSceneCameras`; the first shake is used). The composition adapter turns a spec's camera moves into the scene camera; item depth (parallax) is not applied (warning).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Owner FINAL1 brief, Step 3. One transform in the shared draw path keeps preview and export identical.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Approved                            |

### D-302 — FIX5 construction and preview processing (2026-10-24, proposed)

Offline composition construction stages the existing validated semantic commands without per-command project validation/history snapshots; only its final project is published, after full validation. Draft reads are frozen copies. Yield every 24 layout commands and between scene effects; restore deterministic UUID scope before every yield. Ordinary editor Command Bus transactions retain their existing semantics.
Preview-only processing may downscale reusable image/shape passes to 921,600 pixels and retain up to 24 MiB per canvas. Text, video, unknown/animated image formats and backdrop blends bypass that cache. Export bypasses both budget and cache. Pixel parity and browser performance are not claimed until browser proof passes.

| D-320 | 2026-10-25 | Local projects (PRJ-015, PRJ-016, APP-006 to APP-008, PRJ-010, PRJ-017). `src/persistence/projects.ts`: one IndexedDB record per project (`aive-projects`/`projects`: primary JSON, last good backup, a damaged primary kept once, a 192 px poster, updated and opened times); localStorage keeps only the open project's id and a session flag. The single localStorage slot of earlier versions is copied in once and removed only after the copy is written; a newer-schema slot or record is never overwritten. Without IndexedDB the editor keeps the old single slot. Schema 9 unchanged. | A real user has many projects, and a 5 MB localStorage slot cannot hold a long project. Media bytes stay in the media store (D-004). | Approved |

| D-326 | 2026-10-25 | Phase 1 cloud (docs/PHASE1-PLAN.md; APP-020). Supabase (Auth, Postgres with row-level security, Storage) with no custom server. `supabase/` holds the config, one migration (`projects`: owner, name, schema version, the project JSON as text, a revision bumped by a trigger, thumbnail; `media`: owner and fingerprint; owner-only policies on both, nothing for `anon`; a private `media` bucket, files at `<owner>/<fingerprint>`, owner-folder policies) and SQL policy tests. `src/cloud/backend.ts` is the interface (`CloudBackend`), `src/cloud/supabase.ts` its Supabase implementation, loaded on the first account click; `src/core` is unchanged. The account slot (`src/ui/cloud-ui.ts`) logs in or signs up by email and password (OAuth is in the interface for Google and the Phase 2 connector, not wired to a button), saves the project JSON (references only, D-004) and uploads only the media fingerprints the cloud lacks, saves over a linked cloud project only at the revision it last saw (otherwise asks to save as new; nothing is overwritten), and opens a cloud project as a new local project after downloading its missing media into the media store; a newer schema is refused and not changed. Links between local and cloud projects are a browser setting (`aive.cloudLinks`). Without `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` and `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, or with the server down, the slot says so and local saving is untouched. New dependency `@supabase/supabase-js` 2.117.3 (MIT; about 650 KB unpacked for the package, 9.1 MB with its five `@supabase/*` parts; a lazy chunk, so the first load does not grow); alternative considered: hand-written fetch calls to the Auth, PostgREST and Storage APIs (more code to keep in step with Supabase, PKCE and token refresh by hand). | Owner Phase 1 brief: local only, no spending; the database rules protect the data even if the editor had a bug. | Approved |

The browser test (e2e/phase1-cloud.spec.ts) runs the real editor and the real `@supabase/supabase-js` against a stand-in HTTP API (e2e/cloud-fake.ts) that applies the same owner rule. | D-329 | 2026-10-26 | Phase 1 library catalog format (P1-04, P1-05; `src/library-client/format.ts`, shared by the editor and the owner pipeline). Static files in a catalog folder that stands in for the CDN: `manifest.json` = `{signed, signature, keyId}` where `signed` is the exact manifest text that was signed (`format`, `catalogVersion`, `generatedAt`, optional `rolledBackTo`, and per category the index `url`, `sha256` and `count`); `versions/<n>.json` keeps every signed manifest and `versions/<n>.items.json` what it listed; everything else is `files/<sha256>.<ext>`, immutable. Six categories: elements (shapes except flowchart, backgrounds, transitions, pictures, videos), draw (flowchart shapes), text, templates (with the taxonomy), audio, styles (themes, Creator Pack styles, motion and presets). Each entry: id, kind, name {en, hi}, tags, licence and credit, itemVersion, thumbUrl, a blur data URL (at most 1,600 characters), size, the payload (inline under 8 KB, else a file), heavy media's poster, proxy and full file (with width, height, duration), and the owner fields hidden, featured, order, planKey (null), publishAt, unpublishAt. Payloads are the existing records (`LibraryItem`, the editor's `Theme`, Creator Pack items), so nothing new is invented for the content. An unchanged index keeps its file; a rollback is a new, higher version that re-points to the old version's own index files, so a client may refuse any version lower than the one it has (replay protection). | Owner Phase 1 library brief, Part B. Signing the exact text avoids any JSON canonicalisation question. | Approved |
| D-330 | 2026-10-26 | The owner's data store is JSON files (`owner-data/items.json`, written atomically; files kept once by SHA-256 under `owner-data/files/`). `node:sqlite` exists only from Node 22 (behind an experimental warning), and the project and CI run Node 20 (`/opt/node20` here, D-018); `node -e "require('node:sqlite')"` fails there. | Brief P1-12: node:sqlite if the runtime has it, else JSON files. | Approved |
| D-331 | 2026-10-26 | Publish gate (P1-07; `owner/catalog/gate.ts`). Inputs: Creator Pack files (.json or .txt), media files (wav, mp3, ogg, m4a, png, jpg, webp, mp4, webm, at most 500 MB) with an optional `<file>.meta.json` (id, name, tags, licence), folders and zips (`owner/catalog/zip.ts`, node:zlib). A pack goes through `checkPack` (with the editor's FX ids) and then the editor's own render checks (`runtimeCheck` from src/ui/creator-checks.ts) in Playwright's headless Chromium, on a page served by a private Vite server (`owner/gate/harness.html`, its own random port, no config file). Media are decoded there: pictures get a 320 px PNG poster and an 8 px blur; sounds a waveform and a 12 kHz mono WAV proxy; videos a poster frame and a 320 px VP9 proxy at 100 kbit/s without sound (left out when it is not smaller than the file). Media without a licence are refused. If the browser cannot start, the gate fails closed and nothing is published. Every rejection is one plain sentence (field paths from the pack checker are reworded). Pack items get the id `<packId>-<itemId>` in the shared library, so one pack never overwrites another's item; the same id at the same content is skipped. | Owner Phase 1 library brief, P1-07. The real gate is proven by tests/library/gate-browser.test.ts. | Approved |
| D-332 | 2026-10-26 | Catalog signing (P1-06). Ed25519: signed with node:crypto, verified in the editor with WebCrypto. Checked first in the e2e browser: Chromium 153 (`HeadlessChrome/153.0.8010.12`) imports a raw Ed25519 key and verifies a good signature (true) and rejects a changed message (false), so ECDSA P-256 was not needed. `npm run library:keys` writes the private key (PKCS #8 PEM, mode 600) only to `owner/keys/` (git-ignored) and the raw public key (base64) beside it; the key id is the first 16 hex characters of the public key's SHA-256. The editor takes the public key at build time; a missing or wrong signature, or another key id, rejects the catalog and keeps the cache. | Owner brief P1-06. | Approved |
| D-333 | 2026-10-26 | Seed (P1-09). `npm run library:seed` publishes catalog version 1 from the audit's "moves" list (reports/PHASE1-LIB-AUDIT.md): the 200 Starter Pack items, the 24 font combinations, the 8 themes, the 12 sounds (WAV, waveform, proxy) and the 3 Creator Pack style examples: 247 items. Its sources now live in `library/seed/` (starter-pack-1.json from `scripts/build-library.mjs`, themes.ts, text-combinations.ts, sound-pack.ts). It is deterministic (a fixed time; Ed25519 signatures are deterministic) and idempotent (a second run changes nothing). The seed is the project's own, already tested content, so it is checked by the schemas but does not run the headless render gate; owner uploads always do. Every seed item's licence is "Original, made for AI-Native, no outside assets". | Owner brief P1-09. | Approved |
| D-334 | 2026-10-26 | Owner library manager (P1-12 to P1-16; `owner/`, owner/README.md). Its own server on node:http with no framework, run through Vite's module loader (`owner/run.mjs`, like `pack:check`), bound to 127.0.0.1 (port 4300), with no CORS headers and a Host check (DNS rebinding). One owner account made by `npm run owner:create` (no sign-up route); data in JSON files (D-330). Security: scrypt (N 16384, r 8, p 1), TOTP (RFC 6238, SHA-1, 30 s, 6 digits, one step of drift), 5 failures lock the account for 15 minutes (the right details are refused while locked), constant-time compares, CSP `default-src 'none'` with `script-src 'self'`, X-Frame-Options DENY, nosniff, no-referrer, COOP, no-store; a CSRF token in a header plus a same-origin Origin check on every change; the session cookie is HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, 8 hours (Secure with `OWNER_SECURE_COOKIE=1` behind HTTPS); unpublish, delete, rollback and key rotation need the password and a fresh code from the last 5 minutes (`OWNER_REAUTH_SECONDS`; the e2e test uses 1 s to see the prompt); every action, failed ones included, is a row of an append-only, SHA-256-chained audit log (no route edits a row; an edit on disk is found). The UI is one plain TypeScript file transpiled at start with the project's `typescript` (no bundler), on the editor's design tokens, with English and Hindi labels. `npm run owner` starts the manager and the read-only catalog server (port 4310, GET and HEAD only, catalog files only) together and seeds an empty catalog first. Passkeys, Cloudflare Access or a VPN, HTTPS and a real CDN are launch-gate deployment steps, not built now. | Owner Phase 1 library brief, Part C. | Approved |

The editor's Vite config adds `ownerGuard` (`vite.library.ts`): its dev server answers 404 for `/owner/`, `/owner-data/`, `/catalog-out/` and the same paths through `/@fs/`. A unit test fails if any file under `src/` imports from `owner/`, and, when `dist/` exists, if the built editor contains the owner app's routes, cookie or screens; `npm run build` runs `scripts/assert-editor-bundle.mjs` on every build (Part D). The owner app may import the editor's validated formats (the catalog format, `checkPack`, the render checks), never the other way round. | Brief P1-11: the manager must never be imported by, bundled into or reachable from the editor. | Approved |
| D-336 | 2026-10-26 | Editor catalog client (P1-20, P1-21). `src/library-client/`: the signed manifest is fetched with its ETag (`no-cache`, 304 answers keep everything), its Ed25519 signature is checked with the build-time public key before anything is used, a catalog older than the saved one is refused, each changed category index is fetched and its SHA-256 checked, then the indexes and the manifest are written to IndexedDB (`aive-library`, indexes first, the manifest last, so a cut-off write keeps the old copy). Payload files, posters and proxies go to the media store (`catalog/<sha256>`, `media/<sha32>`). A page starts from the saved copy at once and refreshes in idle time; with no saved copy it waits for the catalog once; with neither it uses a tiny bundled fallback pack (four shapes and one gradient, plus the Text tab's three built-in styles and the Clean Light theme), and the panels say why while still showing the cards. Every problem is one plain sentence. The address is `VITE_LIBRARY_CATALOG_URL` (the dev server's own `/__catalog/` in `npm run dev`); dev and e2e pages may override it (`__AIVE_LIBRARY_CATALOG__`). | Owner Phase 1 library brief, Part D. | Approved |
| D-337 | 2026-10-26 | Catalog media in a project (P1-25, P1-27). A catalog sound, video or picture becomes an ordinary asset at once (`engine.library`, not an undo step) that names its FULL file (`media/<sha32 of the full file>`, id `media-<16>`), plus `catalogProxy`, `catalogPoster` and `catalog: {id, itemVersion}` in its metadata (schema 9 unchanged). Until the full bytes are in this browser the canvas draws the decoded poster and the frame provider and audio decoder read the proxy; the full file is fetched two frames after the placing step and swapped in place: the proxy keeps drawing until the full file has its first frame (no blink, no size change, the layer is untouched), then the audio is decoded again. A failed download leaves the item placed and shows a toast with Retry. Reconnect media skips catalog assets (they fetch their own file). Because the project keeps its own asset record and layers, a catalog rollback or unpublish never changes a project. | Brief P1-25, P1-26, P1-27. | Approved |

The panels read the client in the old `LibraryManifest` shape: Templates, Elements (shapes, graphics, and a new Videos and photos strip for catalog media), Draw's flowchart shapes (in Elements' Flowchart section), Text (with the combinations), Audio (sound effects), Theme (built-in themes registered from the catalog) and AI (catalog styles, motion and presets listed with the user's own as `catalog/<id>`; Rename and Delete do not apply to them). | D-339 | 2026-10-26 | Instant insert (P1-24). A card that shows makes its item local: catalog media prefetch their poster (decoded) and proxy; text and template cards load the fonts their text uses (built from the item's own insert commands through `engine.preview`); every card does the same on pointer enter and down. A catalogue (Google) font that failed is asked for again after 30 s, not on every edit. Proof (e2e, CDP-throttled network, 1.5 s latency, 20 KB/s): for Elements, Draw, Text, Audio, Templates, a video and a photo, by click and by drag, no request is made between the release and the first frame drawn with the new item, and that frame is the first one drawn after the release. The sandbox's software-rendered frames take 80 to 290 ms; the times are recorded in the test output, not claimed as a budget for real hardware. | Brief P1-24, P1-25. | Approved |
